0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{j_{\ast}W}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right]\right]=[V,W].

∎

When it comes to minimal primes, there is no right adjoint on the
whole subcategory. However, if
N
is a
\left(\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\right)
-module which is torsion in the sense that
L_{\mathfrak{g}}N\simeq 0
, then we may take

f(N)=\left[\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.56248pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 31.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.46248pt\raise-14.84721pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.20001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{\Large$\circ$}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.06248pt\raise-24.19443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-7.56248pt\raise-31.13887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.96248pt\raise-31.13887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.20001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{\Large$\circ$}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.56248pt\raise-31.13887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 31.56248pt\raise-31.13887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{wqce}}

Lemma 3.4
.

When
L_{\mathfrak{g}}N\simeq 0
, the object
f(N)
is equivalent to an object
in the image of
a
. Indeed,
f(N)
is
the image of
N
itself viewed as a
\mathbbm{1}
-module. The object
f(-)
has the property

\mathrm{Hom}(f(M),f(N))=\mathrm{Hom}(M,N)
and
[f(M),f(N)]=[M,N]
.

Proof.

We may calculate

a(N)=\left[\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 30.3264pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 58.97026pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{\mathfrak{g}}N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.57858pt\raise-16.18054pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.20001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{\Large$\circ$}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.17859pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-30.3264pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\otimes N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.7264pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.20001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{\Large$\circ$}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.3264pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 54.3264pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{j_{*}L_{\mathfrak{g}}N}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right]

Since
L_{\mathfrak{g}}N\simeq 0
, we have that
a(N)\simeq f(\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\otimes N)
. As
\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\otimes N\simeq N
the result follows as the object is weakly extended.

The statement about maps between
f(M)
and
f(N)
is clear due to the fact that the diagrams are concentrated at a single vertex.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

These
f(-)
and
e(-)
objects are very useful since they correspond
to two layers given by the dimension filtration. That is, the objects
e(-)
are ones
supported on the prime
\mathfrak{g}
, while the
f(-)
are supported on the
closed points
\mathfrak{m}
. In particular, these objects build
all
\mathrm{wqce}
objects. Indeed, for an arbitrary object

X=\left[\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.56248pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 31.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.99025pt\raise-14.75pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.20001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{\Large$\circ$}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.59026pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-7.56248pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.02411pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.20001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{\Large$\circ$}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.68575pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 31.68575pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{wqce}}

there is a natural map
X\longrightarrow e(V)
corresponding to the identity on

V
and this gives a cofibre sequence

(7)

\displaystyle f(N^{\prime})\to X\to e(V),

where
N^{\prime}=\mathrm{fibre}(N\longrightarrow L_{\mathfrak{g}}N)
.

Theorem 3.6
.

[
3
, 9.C]

Every object of
R_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{ad}}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
is equivalent to an
object of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{wqce}}
and hence

\mathcal{C}\simeq_{Q}R_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{ad}}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\simeq_{Q}\mathcal{L}\mathrm{im}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}).

Proof.

By
[
13
, 3.2]
the strict homotopy limit is the
cellularization of the diagram category with respect to

\mathrm{wqce}
. We have right Bousfield localized with respect to the apparently smaller subcategory of objects coming from
\mathcal{C}
, so it suffices to show they
generate the same subcategory.

Suppose then that
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{wqce}}
, and use the notation above for
its values. We must show that
X
is trivial
in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
if and only if it is cellularly trivial. It is obvious that
if
X
is trivial then it is cellularly trivial. Suppose then that
X
is
cellularly trivial in the sense that
[a(g),X]_{\ast}=0
for all

g\in\mathcal{G}
. It follows that
[a(A),X]_{\ast}=0
for all
A\in\mathcal{C}
.

Now consider the cofibre sequence (
7
). Since
[f(N^{\prime}),e(V)]_{*}=0
we see that

[N^{\prime},N^{\prime}]_{*}=[f(N^{\prime}),f(N^{\prime})]_{*}=[f(N^{\prime}),X]_{*}=0

so
N^{\prime}\simeq 0
and it follows that
X\simeq e(V)
. But now

[V,V]_{*}=[e(V),e(V)]_{*}=[e(V),X]_{*}=0

so that
V\simeq 0
. Hence
X\simeq 0
as required.

∎

Remark 3.7
.

3.6
shows that
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{ad}}
generates the localizing subcategory

\mathrm{wqce}
. Since
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{ad}}
is a set of small objects, it shows that
cellularization with respect to
\mathrm{wqce}
exists and
R_{\mathrm{wqce}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}=R_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{ad}}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
. To avoid referring to any particular generators, we
henceforth write
R_{\mathrm{wqce}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
for the adelic model of
\mathcal{C}
.

4.
The separated and complete models

In this section we construct both the separated and complete models from

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
by taking a left localization of a cellularization of

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
. We will introduce the relevant localizations and then prove the following theorem.

Theorem 4.1
.

(Adelic, separated and complete models)

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a 1-dimensional Noetherian model category. Then there are Quillen equivalences

\mathcal{C}\simeq_{Q}R_{\mathrm{wqce}}\mathcal{C}_{ad}\simeq_{Q}L_{\Pi}R_{\mathrm{wqcie}}\mathcal{C}_{ad}\simeq_{Q}L_{\Lambda}R_{\mathrm{wqc}}\mathcal{C}_{ad}.

The outline for this section is as follows. In Subsection
4.A
we will introduce the relevant
classes for cellularization, in Subsection
4.B
we
introduce the left localizations. Subsection
4.C

details the strategy, and then in Subsection
4.D
we
assemble the pieces to prove the theorem.

4.A.
Classes of cofibrant objects

We introduce three classes of objects which will be classes of
cofibrant objects in our models. To show they can be the class of
cofibrant objects we
need to establish they are generated by a set of small objects.
All are weakly quasi-coherent (
\mathrm{wqc}
, a condition on the horizontal base change) but then we
require additional conditions on the vertical base change (either
extendedness or the somewhat weaker condition of idempotent
extendedness). This gives the hierarchy

\mathrm{wqc}\supseteq\mathrm{wqcie}\supseteq\mathrm{wqce}.

4.A.1.
Weakly quasi-coherent objects

In Section
3.B
we proved that the class of
\mathrm{wqce}
objects
was generated by the set
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{ad}}
(see Remark
3.7
).
We now prove
\mathrm{wqc}
is also generated by a set of small objects.
Since we are working stably, it is enough to show that the
acyclic objects in the cellularization are generated by a set of small
objects.

We suppose
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces V}
is
\mathrm{wqc}
(i.e.,
L_{\mathfrak{g}}N\simeq Q
) and then form

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q}

where
N^{\prime}
is constructed as the pullback. As we have assumed that

\mathcal{C}
(and hence
(L_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathbbm{1})\text{-mod}_{\mathcal{C}}
) is right proper, it follows that
L_{\mathfrak{g}}N^{\prime}\simeq j_{\ast}V
. In particular, the back face is a
\mathrm{wqce}
object. Therefore the mapping cone of this morphism of diagrams is of the form
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces 0}

where
P\simeq L_{\mathfrak{g}}M
. It remains to observe
any such object can be
\otimes
-built from the small
\mathrm{wqc}
object
\textstyle{\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{\mathfrak{g}}\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces 0}
.

4.A.2.
Modules over product rings

We are considering modules
M
over product rings
\prod_{i}R_{i}
. Using
the projection
p_{i}:\prod_{i}R_{i}\longrightarrow R_{i}
we may define an

R_{i}
-module by extension of scalars

e_{i}M:=R_{i}\otimes_{\prod_{i}R_{i}}M.

This is idempotent in the sense that if we view
e_{i}M
as an
\prod_{i}R_{i}
-module by restriction
e_{i}e_{i}M\cong e_{i}M
since
R_{i}\otimes_{\prod_{i}R_{i}}R_{i}\cong R_{i}
.

Indeed, there is an adjunction

\textstyle{\left(\prod_{i}R_{i}\right)\text{-mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\prod_{i}\left(R_{i}\text{-mod}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\pi}

The right adjoint
\pi
simply takes products of modules, and
the left adjoint
\sigma
is defined by
\sigma(M)_{i}=e_{i}M
.
For a module
M
over
\prod_{i}R_{i}
we write
M^{\Pi}=\pi\sigma M
, and think of the unit of the adjunction
M\longrightarrow M^{\Pi}
as a
form of completion.

4.A.3.
Weakly quasi-coherent idempotent extended objects

We now consider the special case of product rings where the factors are
the completions
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}
.

Definition 4.2
.

A map
V\longrightarrow Q
of modules over

L_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathbbm{1}\longrightarrow L_{\mathfrak{g}}\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}
is
idempotent
extended
if the idempotent pieces
e_{\mathfrak{m}}j_{\ast}V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}e_{\mathfrak{m}}Q
of
the base change map are isomorphisms for all closed points
\mathfrak{m}
. It
is
weakly idempotent extended
if the maps are weak
equivalences, and we write
\mathrm{wqcie}
for the collection of all weakly
quasi coherent, weakly idempotent extended objects.

Since passage to idempotent pieces preserves weak equivalences, a
weakly extended object is weakly idempotent extended and
\mathrm{wqcie}\supseteq\mathrm{wqce}
.

By similar arguments to Section
3.B

and
4.A.1
we
see that
\mathrm{wqcie}
is
the class of cofibrant objects in suitable cellularizations with
respect to a set of small objects.

We suppose
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces V}
is
\mathrm{wqcie}
and then once again form

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q}

As before, the mapping cone is of the form
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces 0}

where
P\simeq L_{\mathfrak{g}}M
, and we also see that
M
is
\Pi
-trivial in the sense that
M
is not necessarily equivalent to 0, but
M^{\Pi}\simeq 0
.

It then remains to observe that any
\Pi
-trivial object is
\otimes
-built from the

\Pi
-trivial object

T:=\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}/\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}
. This
may not be small for all modules but
[T,\bigoplus_{i}N_{i}]=\bigoplus_{i}[T,N_{i}]
if
N_{i}
is
\Pi
-trivial since
[T,N]=[\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1},N]
when
N
is

\Pi
-trivial.

4.B.
Bousfield localizations

We now explore the appropriate left Bousfield
localizations corresponding to the right Bousfield localizations
R_{\mathrm{wqce}},R_{\mathrm{qcie}}
and

R_{\mathrm{wqc}}
as in Subsection
2.E
.

Given an object
E=(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.56248pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 31.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Q}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 69.46803pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces V}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.46803pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces)
of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
, we may consider the
set
E
-we of those maps
f
so that
E\otimes f
is a weak equivalence. We
note that this depends only on the objects
V,Q
and
N
appearing in the diagram and not on the base-change
maps between them. Since we work stably, it is enough to consider the set
\langle E\rangle
of

E
-acyclics.

4.B.1.
The
\Pi
Bousfield localization

We first consider a localization designed to ensure the bottom left
object is a product of modules for the individual factors

\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}
, to be used for the separated model. This will
be Bousfield localization with respect to

\Pi=\{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 12.50696pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.50696pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.50696pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 36.50696pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{\mathfrak{g}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 96.81259pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathbbm{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.81259pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\;|\;\mathfrak{m}\mbox{ a closed point}\}

We remark that the
\mathrm{wqce}
-cofibrant replacement is

\Gamma_{\mathrm{wqce}}(\Pi)\simeq\{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 34.0122pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.0122pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\times L_{\mathfrak{g}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 58.0122pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 58.0122pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{\mathfrak{g}}\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 134.83179pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathbbm{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 110.83179pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\;|\;\mathfrak{m}\mbox{ a closed point}\},

where
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}=\prod_{\mathfrak{n}\neq\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{n}}\mathbbm{1}
.

4.B.2.
The
\Lambda
Bousfield localization

Now we consider a localization designed to ensure the bottom left
object is a product of complete modules for the individual factors

\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}
, to be used in the complete model. This
will be Bousfield localization with respect to

\Lambda=\{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.1389pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.1389pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K_{\mathfrak{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.1389pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 35.1389pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 70.1389pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathbbm{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.13892pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\;|\;\mathfrak{m}\mbox{ a closed point}\}

where
K_{\mathfrak{m}}
is a Koszul object with support
\overline{\{\mathfrak{m}\}}
as used to define completion.

We remark that the
\mathrm{wqce}
-cofibrant replacement is

\Gamma_{\mathrm{wqce}}(\Lambda)\simeq\{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 39.54868pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-39.54868pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K_{\mathfrak{m}}\times L_{\mathfrak{g}}\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.54868pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 63.54868pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{\mathfrak{g}}\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 140.36826pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathbbm{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.36826pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\;|\;\mathfrak{m}\mbox{ a closed point}\}.

It is clear that the set
\Pi
generates a localizing subcategory
containing
\Lambda
. As such it follows that
L_{\Pi}=L_{\Pi\coprod\Lambda}
, and there
is a natural transformation
L_{\Pi}\longrightarrow L_{\Lambda}
.

4.C.
Quillen equivalences of left and right localizations

In this subsection we describe the main strategy of proof for Theorem

4.1
: we provide sufficient conditions for various
composites of left and right Bousfield localizations to be Quillen equivalences. In the next section we will apply these criteria to establish the separated and complete
models.

In fact we will be interested in zig-zags of left Quillen functors

\mathcal{C}\longleftarrow R\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow LR\mathcal{C}.

This zig-zag is obtained by considering the square

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\mathcal{C}}
\textstyle{LR\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox to0.0pt{ .\hss}}

We will give criteria for these localizations to be Quillen
equivalences.

Recall that a left localization
\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow L\mathcal{C}
is a Quillen equivalence if all the
maps that are inverted are already weak equivalences. We observe that it is
enough to check this on weak equivalences between cofibrant
objects, and the cofibrant objects are the same in both categories. Once again, for stable localizations it is enough to check this for acyclics. That is, we must show that if
x
is a
cofibrant object with
x\simeq 0
in
L\mathcal{C}
then in fact
x\simeq 0
in

\mathcal{C}
.

Similarly to show that a right localization
R\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
is a Quillen equivalence we
must show that if
x\longrightarrow y
is a cellular equivalence then it is an
actual equivalence. It is also sufficient to do this for fibrant
objects, and fibrant objects are the same in both categories. In the stable setting it is enough to do it for acyclics. That is, we must show that if
x
is a fibrant object with
x\simeq 0
in
R\mathcal{D}
then in fact
x\simeq 0
in

\mathcal{D}
.

Of particular interest to this section will be those left Quillen functors of the form
LR\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow L\mathcal{D}
. Suppose we have a morphism
f\colon x\longrightarrow y

where
x
is cofibrant in
LR\mathcal{D}
and
y
is fibrant in
L\mathcal{D}
. We must then show that
f
is a weak equivalence in
LR\mathcal{D}
if and only if it is a weak equivalence in
L\mathcal{D}
. We now assume that
L
is a homological localization with respect to some object
E
. Observe that it is enough to check for
x
cofibrant in
R\mathcal{D}
and
y
fibrant in
L\mathcal{D}
that if
f
is an equivalence in
LR\mathcal{D}
then it is an equivalence in
L\mathcal{D}
. In particular, it suffices to show that if
E\otimes x\longrightarrow E\otimes y
is an equivalence in
R\mathcal{D}
then it is an equivalence in
\mathcal{D}
.

4.D.
Cases at hand

For the cofibrant objects we have three candidates
\mathrm{wqce}\subseteq\mathrm{wqcie}\subseteq\mathrm{wqc}
. We showed in Sections

3.B
,

4.A.1
and
4.A.3
that
each of these three classes is the sets of cofibrant objects
in the cellularization with respect to a set of small objects. Accordingly, we can
form right Bousfield localizations with respect to each of them.

For controlling the fibrant objects we wish to use the left Bousfield localizations introduced in
Section

4.B
, namely
L_{\Pi}
and
L_{\Lambda}
.

Assembling the pieces, one may construct the following diagram of left Quillen functors, where the marked equivalences are the things we shall now prove:

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R_{\mathrm{wqc}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R_{\mathrm{wqcie}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R_{\mathrm{wqce}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L_{\Pi}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{\Pi}R_{\mathrm{wqc}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{\Pi}R_{\mathrm{wqcie}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L_{\Pi}R_{\mathrm{wqce}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L_{\Lambda}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}}
\textstyle{L_{\Lambda}R_{\mathrm{wqc}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{\Lambda}R_{\mathrm{wqcie}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L_{\Lambda}R_{\mathrm{wqce}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

In the following discussion we will as usual have an object

\displaystyle x=\left(\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.56248pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 31.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.59026pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-7.56248pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.63748pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 31.63748pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Q}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right)

with nub
N
, and vertex
V
.

To establish the right hand vertical equivalences, since
\Lambda\subseteq\Pi
, it suffices to prove that if a
\mathrm{wqce}
object is
\Lambda
-trivial then it is
trivial. Suppose then that
x
is
\mathrm{wqce}
and moreover

\Lambda
-trivial. Since the vertex
V
is at an initial point of the
diagram, we conclude that
V\simeq 0
. Since the object
x
is weakly extended we have
j_{\ast}V\simeq Q\simeq 0
. Since it is weakly quasi-coherent, it follows that
N
is torsion. The result then follows from the fact that a torsion object
N
with
N\otimes K_{\mathfrak{m}}\simeq 0
for all

\mathfrak{m}
is contractible.

To establish the right hand horizontal equivalence in the middle row
we need to show that if
we have a morphism
x\to y
where
x
is
\mathrm{wqce}
,
y
is
\mathrm{wqcie}
and
x\otimes\pi\longrightarrow y\otimes\pi
is a

\mathrm{wqce}
-cellular equivalence for all
\pi\in\Pi
then it is a
\mathrm{wqcie}
-cellular equivalence.

It is convenient to factor
x\longrightarrow y
as
x\longrightarrow y^{c}\longrightarrow y
where
y^{c}
is

\mathrm{wqce}
(i.e., the cofibrant replacement of
y
in
R_{\mathrm{wqce}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
). Now the map
y^{c}\longrightarrow y
is an equivalence at the vertex,
an i-equivalence at the
L\Lambda\mathbbm{1}
-module, and a

\Pi
-equivalence at the nub, so it is a
\Pi
-equivalence. In other words we may assume both

x
and
y
are
\mathrm{wqce}
, and since we are in the stable setting,
we need only prove for
x

\mathrm{wqce}
that
[k,x\otimes\pi]=0
for
k

\mathrm{wqce}

implies it also holds for
k

\mathrm{wqcie}
. If
k
is
\mathrm{wqcie}
then one checks
that the
\mathrm{wqce}
-cellularization
k^{c}\longrightarrow k
induces an isomorphism in
[-,x\otimes\pi]
for
\pi\in\Pi
. This is true because

[k,x\otimes\pi]=[k\otimes\pi,x\otimes\pi]
, and
k^{c}\longrightarrow k
is
a
\pi
-equivalence.

To establish the lower horizontal equivalences we need to show that if

x
is
\mathrm{wqce}
, and
y
is
\mathrm{wqc}
and
x\otimes\pi\longrightarrow y\otimes\pi
is a

\mathrm{wqce}
-cellular equivalence then it is a
\mathrm{wqc}
-cellular equivalence. It
is once again convenient to factor
x\longrightarrow y
as
x\longrightarrow y^{c}\longrightarrow y
where
y^{c}
is

\mathrm{wqce}
. Now the map
y^{c}\longrightarrow y
is an equivalence at the vertex, and a

\Lambda
-equivalence at the
\Lambda\mathbbm{1}

spot, so it is a
\Lambda
-equivalence. In other words we may assume both

x
and
y
are
\mathrm{wqce}
, and since we are in the stable setting,
we need only prove for
x

\mathrm{wqce}
that
[k,x\otimes\lambda]=0
for
k

\mathrm{wqce}
for all
\lambda\in\Lambda

implies it also holds for
k

\mathrm{wqc}
. If
k
is
\mathrm{wqc}
then the

\mathrm{wqc}
-cellularization
k^{c}\longrightarrow k
induces an isomorphism in
[-,x\otimes\lambda]
.

Indeed, suppose

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle=(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.39583pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.39583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.39583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 32.39583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 70.20485pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces U}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.20486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces),

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle=(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.56248pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 31.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Q}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 69.46803pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces V}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.46803pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces),

\displaystyle\lambda

. Since

X
, and
[L_{\mathfrak{g}}X,N\otimes K_{\mathfrak{m}}]=0
.

This completes the proof of Theorem
4.1
.

4.E.
Bifibrant objects

In summary, we now have three homotopical models:

•

The adelic model –
R_{\mathrm{wqce}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
, with bifibrant objects
\mathrm{wqce}

•

The separated model –
L_{\Pi}R_{\mathrm{wqcie}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
, with bifibrant
objects
\mathrm{wpqcie}
(i.e.,
\mathrm{wqcie}
and the nub equivalent to a product)

•

The complete model –
L_{\Lambda}R_{\mathrm{wqc}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
, with bifibrant
objects
\mathrm{wqc\kappa}
(i.e.,
\mathrm{wqc}
, with the nub complete).

To finish off
Part

I
we will describe explicit constructions of moving between bifibrant replacements of one of these models to another.

To obtain a separated or complete bifibrant object from an adelic bifibrant object
(i.e., a
\mathrm{wqce}
-object
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces V}
) we form the diagram

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{LN\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{N^{\Pi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{LN^{\Pi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{N^{\Lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{LN^{\Lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

where the front right faces define
Q^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime\prime}
as homotopy pushouts
and superscripts
\Pi
and
\Lambda
refer to fibrant replacements. One
sees that the map
Q\longrightarrow Q^{\prime}
is an idempotent equivalence since that holds for

N\longrightarrow N^{\Pi}
and this is preserved by localization.

Alternatively, given a bifibrant model in the complete or separated
model, we need to upgrade the vertical from a map or an
idempotent extended map and to a fully extended map, and these operate by taking homotopy pullbacks on the
back face. The fact that these are homotopy pullbacks was discussed in
the overall introduction.

To go from the complete to the separated
model we first go to the standard model and then to the separated
model.

Part II
Algebraic models

5.
Skeletons and the roadmap

In
Part

II
we show how in certain
algebraic situations we can replace the underlying category
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
of
the model by a more economical skeleton. A skeleton of
a model category is a Quillen equivalent subcategory
\mathcal{S}
in which homotopy
equivalences are closer to isomorphisms, so that the homotopy relation
has less work to do.

5.A.
Skeletons

The simplest case is when the inclusion
i\colon\mathcal{S}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
has a
right
adjoint, that is,
\mathcal{S}
is
coreflective
in
\mathcal{C}
. The subcategory
\mathcal{S}
then inherits completeness and
cocompleteness
from
\mathcal{C}
.
If
the generating sets of cofibrations and acyclic cofibrations of
\mathcal{C}

lie in
\mathcal{S}
there is a left-lifted model structure on

\mathcal{S}
with the same classes of weak equivalences,
cofibrations and fibrations
[
22
]
and the inclusion is a
Quillen equivalence: we say
\mathcal{S}
is a

cellular skeleton
of
\mathcal{C}
.

If the inclusion
i:\mathcal{S}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
has a
left
adjoint, that is,
\mathcal{S}
is
reflective
in
\mathcal{C}
, then
there is a little more to do. The subcategory
\mathcal{S}
again inherits
completeness and cocompleteness from
\mathcal{C}
. For a simple example of this, the
reader can look ahead to Section
6.B
, where we
describe
\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})
as a reflective subcategory of

\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})
.

Under suitable hypotheses, if

\mathcal{C}
is cofibrantly generated
we can then lift the model structure in
\mathcal{C}
along
the right adjoint
i
to obtain a model structure on
\mathcal{S}
.
If the inclusion of
\mathcal{S}
in
\mathcal{C}
is a Quillen equivalence,
we say
\mathcal{S}
is a
local skeleton
of
\mathcal{C}
.

For example, if the left adjoint takes small objects to small objects, and if the unit and
counit are equivalences on generators, we obtain the conclusion from
the Cellularization Principle. However, in our principal examples the left
adjoint does not preserve small objects and we shall see in Section
9.B
that we must argue more directly.

5.B.
The roadmap

We may now describe our strategy. In Part 1 we constructed three
different models for
\mathcal{C}
with underlying category
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
. We are going to
vary the underlying category and find models for
\mathcal{C}
on each one. In
fact we will construct a diagram of adjoint pairs in the pattern

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qce}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{\mathrm{qcie}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qc}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{\mathrm{e}}}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{\mathrm{qc}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{\mathrm{ie}}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{L\kappa}}^{\mathrm{qc}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{\ell^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{\mathrm{qc}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{\mathrm{qc}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{L\kappa}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{\mathrm{qc}}}

The categories
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{L\kappa}}
at the bottom are categories of diagrams
on the cospans
\mathrm{ad},\mathrm{sep}
and
\mathrm{L\kappa}
to be introduced below.
Above each of the three there are subcategories.
In each case, the letter
i
indicates a coreflective inclusion with
right adjoint
\Gamma
(with appropriate subscript). The functors

\Gamma
are introduced in Section
8
.
The horizontal adjoint pairs are induced from adjoint pairs at one
vertex of the cospan by a construction to be described in Subsection

7.B
. We will show that all the categories in the
diagram except
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{L\kappa}}
inherit model structures lifted
from that on
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
.

The diagram serves as a roadmap.
The minimal skeletons of the adelic, separated and complete models are at the tops of the columns.
The fact that vertical inclusions
i
are Quillen equivalences follows
by the cellular skeleton argument above: this only requires us to show that the model
structures are generated by cofibrations and acyclic cofibrations from
the top category in each column. In effect we need to show an object
for which the
\mathrm{qc}
,
\mathrm{qce}
,
\mathrm{qcie}
condition holds weakly is equivalent to a subobject for which is holds strongly.

For the horizontal adjunctions, the categories decrease in size as we
move right. To show that they are inclusions of local skeletons, we
use corresponding generators and show that the unit and counit are
weak equivalences.

5.C.
The equivalences

Here we shall give an overview of the argument: full
details are provided in Section
9
.

If we start from the adelic model
R_{\mathrm{wqce}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}

at the bottom left and the passage to cellular skeletons in the first
column shows

R_{\mathrm{wqce}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}=R_{\mathrm{we}}R_{\mathrm{wqc}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\simeq_{Q}R_{\mathrm{we}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qc}}\simeq_{Q}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qce}},

and the cellular skeleton of the adelic model is
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qce}}
.

If we start from the separated model
L_{\Pi}R_{\mathrm{wqcie}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
we have the equivalences

L_{\Pi}R_{\mathrm{wqcie}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}=L_{\Pi}R_{\mathrm{wie}}R_{\mathrm{wqc}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\simeq}}_{Q}L_{\Pi}R_{\mathrm{wie}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qc}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\simeq}}_{Q}R_{\mathrm{wie}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{\mathrm{qc}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{\simeq}}_{Q}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{\mathrm{qcie}}

where (1) and (3) are cellular skeleton equivalences and (2) is a local
skeleton equivalence. The skeleton of the separated model is
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{\mathrm{qcie}}
.

If we start from the complete model
L_{\Lambda}R_{\mathrm{wqc}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}
we have the equivalences

L_{\Lambda}R_{\mathrm{wqc}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\simeq}}_{Q}L_{\Lambda}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qc}}=L_{\Lambda}L_{\Pi}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qc}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\simeq}}_{Q}L_{\Lambda}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{\mathrm{qc}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{\simeq}}_{Q}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{L\kappa}}^{\mathrm{qc}}

where (1) is a cellular skeleton equivalence, whilst (2) and (3) are local
skeleton equivalences. The skeleton of the complete model is
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{L\kappa}}^{\mathrm{qc}}
.

It remains to introduce the categories and functors appearing in the
roadmap diagram, which we do in Sections
7
and
8
. After this we shall show
that objects with weak conditions are equivalent to ones with strong
conditions. Finally we establish that the unit and counits of the horizontal adjunctions are
derived equivalences on generators: this will be done in Section
9
.

6.
Local skeletons of the nub

As described above, there are two ways of moving to a smaller
skeleton. One of them changes the categories in the diagram and one of them
places restrictions on the objects for a fixed diagram. The change of
categories in the diagram is passage to a local skeleton, and is based on changing the nub category
(\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1})\text{-mod}
to a local skeleton. In this section we
introduce the functors we use to change the nub and in Section

7
we explain how to lift this to diagrams.

6.A.
Pattern

In both cases the argument proceeds as follows. We have an inclusion

i:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}
of a subcategory with a left adjoint
F
, and a
left Bousfield localization
L
of
\mathcal{N}
. In our situation this gives a diagram of
left Quillen functors

\textstyle{\mathcal{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{L\mathcal{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq_{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{F}}

In other words, we have a Quillen pair
F\dashv i
relating
\mathcal{N}
to

\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
, but
F
is also a left Quillen functor from
L\mathcal{N}
and the
same adjoint pair gives a Quillen equivalence
L\mathcal{N}\simeq_{Q}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
.

6.B.
The separated skeleton

Let
R_{\mathfrak{m}}=\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}
in a Noetherian model category
\mathcal{C}
. There are many modules over the product ring
\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}}
, but the
ones we care most about are those which are products of modules over
the individual rings
R_{\mathfrak{m}}
. Using the projective model
structure,

\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})
is a local skeleton of
(\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}})\text{-mod}
.

As in Subsection
4.A.2
, the functor

\sigma:(\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}})\text{-mod}\longrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}).

defined by

\sigma(M)_{\mathfrak{m}}=e_{\mathfrak{m}}M:=R_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}}}M

has right adjoint

\pi:(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})\longrightarrow(\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}})\text{-mod}

defined by

\pi(\{N_{\mathfrak{m}}\})=\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}N_{\mathfrak{m}}.

To see that
\sigma
is the inclusion of a reflective subcategory we
note

N_{\mathfrak{m}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}R_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}}}\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}N_{\mathfrak{m}}

is an isomorphism.

Lemma 6.1
.

The functor
\sigma:(\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}})\text{-mod}\longrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})
is the inclusion of a reflective subcategory and

\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})
is a local skeleton of
L_{\Pi}(\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}})\text{-mod}.

Proof.

We take the projective model structure on
(\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}})\text{-mod}
and similarly on each of the factors of
\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})
. We see that
\sigma\dashv\pi
is a Quillen adjunction,
and we may lift the model structure along the right adjoint.

Indeed, this same adjunction gives a Quillen pair
\overline{\sigma}:L_{\Pi}(\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}})\text{-mod}\longrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})
. One checks directly
that this is a Quillen equivalence.
∎

6.C.
The L-complete skeleton

Unlike the separated skeleton, the L-complete skeleton will only exist under some hypothesis on
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}
. As such, we now restrict to the situation that
R_{\mathfrak{m}}
is a graded commutative Noetherian local ring with maximal
ideal
\mathfrak{m}
. We would like to
replace
R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}
by a skeleton which is complete in some sense.
The first thought is to replace
R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}

by its Bousfield completion
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})

however this is not helpful for the skeleton since the underlying subcategory remains the
same.

It is natural to seek an abelian model. One thinks first of the category of
\mathfrak{m}
-adically
complete modules, but since
\mathfrak{m}
-adic completion is not exact, the
category is not well behaved. Nonetheless, Pol and Williamson have shown the completion
is the derived category of a convenient abelian category.
Writing
L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}
for the 0th left derived functor
L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}

of
\mathfrak{m}
-adic completion, we take the
L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}
-complete modules

M
(those for which the natural transformation
M\longrightarrow L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}M
is an
isomorphism
[
17
]
). The category
L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}\mbox{-$R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}$}
of differential
graded
L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}
-complete modules is a very well behaved reflective
subcategory of
R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}
and gives a local skeleton. The following
result is stated in
[
25
, Appendix A]
,

[
7
, Appendix A]
; both references
have a blanket assumption that their rings are regular and local, but
these assumptions are unnecessary for the given proofs of the following statement.

Lemma 6.2
.

•

The category
L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}\mbox{-$R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}$}
is abelian, and the inclusion
functor
i\colon L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}\mbox{-$R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}$}\hookrightarrow R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}
is exact.

•

The inclusion functor has left adjoint
L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}
.

•

The category
L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}\mbox{-$R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}$}
is complete and cocomplete.

On the basis of this we have an abelian local skeleton of
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})
.

Proposition 6.3

(
[
28
, 6.10]
)
.

There is a cofibrantly generated monoidal model structure on

L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}\mbox{-$R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}$}
where a map is a weak equivalence or fibration if it is when
viewed in
R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}
: the model structure is right-lifted from that in

R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}
.

Moreover, there is a symmetric monoidal Quillen equivalence

L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}:\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})\rightleftarrows L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}\mbox{-$R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}$}:i.

We note that
L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}
does not preserve sequential
colimits, so this requires a direct proof rather than using the
general right-lifting criterion.

7.
The adelic, separated and L-complete categories

Having described two left adjoints at the nub category, we need to explain how
to this may be extended to the whole cospan. In Section
9.B
we describe how to equip these categories with relevant model structures.

7.A.
Quasi-coherent diagram categories

We will name and display the left adjoints, with notation
for the right adjoints following from it, so that if
f:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a left adjoint the right adjoint will be called
f^{r}
.

We start from a cospan of left adjoints, but since we focus on
quasi-coherent objects, the categories do not
play equal roles, and the notation reflects this. The cospan of left
adjoints is the diagram
\alpha=(\mathcal{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle v}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{Q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\longleftarrow}}\mathcal{V})
, where
h
and
v
stand for ‘horizontal’
and ‘vertical’ and we have the corresponding category

\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}=\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{N},h)={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\left(\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise of diagrams. The vertical functor
v:\mathcal{V}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
will remain
constant throughout the discussion so is not recorded in the
notation. Given a left adjoint
F:\mathcal{N}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
we may form a new
cospan

F_{*}\alpha=(\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F^{r}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{Q}\longleftarrow\mathcal{V})

where the horizontal
h
has been replaced by the composite
hF^{r}
. We
note that the horizontal of
F_{*}\alpha
is usually not a left
adjoint. Nonetheless, we can consider the category of diagrams

\mathcal{C}_{F_{*}\alpha}=\mathcal{C}_{F_{*}\alpha}(\mathcal{N}^{\prime},hF^{r})={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\left(\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 52.38136pt\raise-37.10611pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{h}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.65279pt\raise-31.67557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.65279pt\raise-31.67557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{Q}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right)

7.B.
Quasi-coherent pushout along a left adjoint

Our main focus is the category

\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}^{qc}=\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}^{qc}(\mathcal{N},h)={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\left(\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 30.8889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.6389pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.6389pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.47858pt\raise-36.76385pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{h}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.35pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.6pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{\Large\textbullet}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{Q}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right)

of
\mathrm{qc}
objects. Since the base change along
h
is an isomorphism, we
may think of a
\mathrm{qc}
object

Y=\left(\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.56248pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 31.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.59026pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.59026pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-7.56248pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.19998pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.6pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{\Large\textbullet}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.63748pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 31.63748pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Q}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right)

as the nub
N
together with the additional structure of a vertex
V

and a map
i:v(V)\longrightarrow h(N)
in the splicing category
\mathcal{Q}
.

We describe how a left adjoint
F:\mathcal{N}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
induces comparisons between
\mathrm{qc}
diagrams
with different nubs.

Lemma 7.1
.

A left adjoint
F:\mathcal{N}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
induces a
left adjoint
F_{*}:\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}^{qc}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{F_{*}\alpha}^{qc}
. If
F
is the inclusion of a
reflective subcategory, so is
F_{*}
.

Proof.

The left adjoint
F_{*}
is defined by

F_{*}Y=\left(\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 48.9213pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.94907pt\raise-15.69443pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.94907pt\raise-23.94443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.38887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 45.5058pt\raise-31.38887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{hN\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.94907pt\raise-47.11108pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{h\eta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.94907pt\raise-55.38885pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-11.47221pt\raise-62.8333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{FN\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.07222pt\raise-62.8333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.6pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{\Large\textbullet}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.47221pt\raise-62.8333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 35.47221pt\raise-62.8333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{hF^{r}FN}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right)

where
\eta:N\longrightarrow F^{r}FN
is the unit of the adjunction. The right
adjoint
F_{*}^{r}=F^{*}
is defined by

F^{*}Y^{\prime}=\left(\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.08911pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 48.62846pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.05902pt\raise-16.00891pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.05902pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-15.08911pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F^{r}N^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.68912pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.6pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{\Large\textbullet}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.08911pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 39.08911pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{hF^{r}N^{\prime}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right)

The unit
\hat{\eta}:Y\longrightarrow F^{*}F_{*}Y
of the adjunction is given by the
diagram

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{hN\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{hN\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{r}FN\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{hF^{r}FN}

The counit
\hat{\epsilon}:F_{*}F^{*}Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow Y^{\prime}
of the adjunction is given by the
diagram

\textstyle{V^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{hF^{r}N^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{FF^{r}N^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{hF^{r}FF^{r}N^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{hF^{r}N^{\prime}}

where commutativity of the triangle is given by the triangular identity of the
original adjunction. The triangular identities for

\hat{\eta}
and
\hat{\epsilon}
follow directly from the triangular
identities of
\eta
and
\epsilon
at the nub.
∎

7.C.
The separated cospan

The starting point is the adelic diagram
\alpha=\mathrm{ad}
, so that in

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qc}}
, the nub is a module over the product ring
\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}}
where
R_{\mathfrak{m}}=\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}
. In the
expanded notation,

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qc}}=\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qc}}((\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}})\text{-mod},L_{\mathfrak{g}}).

We now use the left adjoint

\sigma:(\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}R_{\mathfrak{m}})\text{-mod}\longrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})

from Subsection
6.B
.

Definition 7.2
.

The category
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is the category of sections of the
separated cospan
:

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}=\mathcal{C}_{\sigma_{*}ad}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\left(\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.89594pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 77.15987pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(L_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathbbm{1})\text{-mod}_{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 106.54883pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.63542pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j_{\ast}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 106.54883pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-37.89594pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\left[\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\text{-mod}_{\mathcal{C}}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.8633pt\raise-38.37778pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{L_{\mathfrak{g}}\pi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.89594pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 61.89594pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(L_{\mathfrak{g}}\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\right)\text{-mod}_{\mathcal{C}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right)

Unpacking the definition, the horizontal functor
L_{\mathfrak{g}}\pi
takes a collection of modules

\{M_{\mathfrak{m}}\}_{\mathfrak{m}}
to the module

L_{\mathfrak{g}}\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}M_{\mathfrak{m}}
, so that an object of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is a diagram of the form

X=\left[\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 20.46532pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 44.46532pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.42433pt\raise-24.33334pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-20.46532pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\{M_{\mathfrak{m}}\}_{\mathfrak{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.51982pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 44.51982pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\right]

Its base change maps are
j_{\ast}U\to P
and
L_{\mathfrak{g}}\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}M_{\mathfrak{m}}\to P
.

Our model is built from the
\mathrm{qc}
objects
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{\mathrm{qc}}
in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. The
construction of Section
7.B
shows that
the
\sigma\dashv\pi
adjunction induces an adjunction with left adjoint

\sigma_{*}:\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qc}}=\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{qc}}(L_{\mathfrak{g}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{\mathrm{qc}}(L_{\mathfrak{g}}\pi)=\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{\mathrm{qc}}.

7.D.
The L-complete cospan

We use the
L
-complete local skeleton of Subsection
6.C

and the constructions of Section
7.B
to
replace the nub
\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\text{-mod})
by a complete
category, but we need to restrict the categories to the appropriate
context.

We restrict attention to the special case where individual categories

\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{m}})
are algebraic in a strong sense.

Definition 7.3
.

We say that
\mathcal{C}
is
formally algebraic
if, for each closed
point
\mathfrak{m}
,
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}
is formal in the sense that we can find a Noetherian graded commutative local ring
(R_{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{m})
and a Quillen equivalence
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\text{-mod}_{\mathcal{C}}\simeq R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}
.

Note that we have abused notation by using
\mathfrak{m}

to refer to both the Balmer prime
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathrm{Spc}^{\omega}(\mathcal{C})
on the
left, and the algebraic prime
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathrm{Spec}(R_{\mathfrak{m}})
on the
right.

Example 7.4
.

(i)

The most obvious example is if
\mathcal{C}=R\text{-mod}
of (chain complexes of)
R
-modules for a 1-dimensional
commutative Noetherian domain, so that all the completions come from a
single ring: in this case
(R_{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{m})=(R_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\wedge},\mathfrak{m})
. The results are valuable
even in this case.

(ii)

We will also consider the example when
\mathcal{C}
consists of
(chain complexes of)
quasi-coherent sheaves over a curve
C
. In this case
\mathfrak{m}
runs
through closed points and
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathcal{O}_{C}\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{C})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\wedge}
is a skyscraper sheaf, and we may take
R_{\mathfrak{m}}=\Gamma(C;(\mathcal{O}_{C})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\wedge})
.

As in Subsection
6.C
, we let
L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}\mbox{-$R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}$}

denote the category of differential
graded
L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}
-complete modules. We consider the product

\ell:\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})\longrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{m}}(L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}-R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod})

of the individual left adjoints

L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}:R_{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-mod}\longrightarrow L_{0}^{\mathfrak{m}}-\Lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathbbm{1}\text{-mod}.

We may now define a skeleton of the complete model.

Definition 7.5
.

Suppose
\mathcal{C}
is formally algebraic. We
define the
L
-complete cospan

\mathcal{C}_{\ell_{*}ad}
and the associated
category of diagrams

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{L\kappa}}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\left(\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 41.25879pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 85.39252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(L_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathbbm{1})\text{-mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 111.6773pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.63542pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j_{\ast}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 111.6773pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-41.25879pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 43.89067pt\raise-38.41666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{L_{\mathfrak{g}}\ell}$}}}\kern
